  [raja-Ju-Ma] Raja Yoga (Kendra–Trikona link)  planets=Ma,Ju
      Mars (an angle lord) and Jupiter (a trine lord) stand in mutual aspect. The classical engine of rank: when the ruler of a fortune house (1st, 5th or 9th) joins the ruler of a corner house (1st, 4th, 7th or 10th), status is lifted, and the results ripen in the main periods (dashas) of the two planets involved.

      The 5th lord Sun joins the 2nd lord Venus — fortune's lord touching the wealth axis. Money arrives through merit, timing or backing rather than grind alone.
  [dhana-9-11] Dhana Yoga (9th–11th lords)  planets=Ju,Sa
      The 9th lord Jupiter stands in mutual aspect with the 11th lord Saturn — fortune's lord touching the wealth axis. Money arrives through merit, timing or backing rather than grind alone.
  [parivartana-Ju-Me] Parivartana Yoga (Dainya)  planets=Me,Ju
      Mercury and Jupiter sit in each other's signs — a parivartana, or mutual exchange, linking the 3rd and 6th with the 9th and 12th. An exchange fuses two houses: neither can be read without the other, and whatever happens to one shows up in the other. A difficult house is inside the exchange, so its results arrive the hard way — obstruction first, resolution afterwards. The tradition still counts it a yoga: what it costs early it tends to return as capability later.
  [sunapha] Sunapha Yoga  planets=Ve
      Venus occupies the sign after the Moon. Sunapha builds self-made standing: intelligence applied to earning, wealth accumulated by your own effort rather than inherited, and a reputation that grows on results. What Venus governs is where that earning tends to concentrate.

--- personality ---
  headline: Aries rising, Sagittarius Moon in Mula, Capricorn Sun — a personality led by Mercury and tested through Jupiter.
  strongest=Me weakest=Ju
  == Your rising sign — how you come across
     [1] Your rising sign (the 'Lagna' — the sign that was coming up over the eastern horizon at the moment you were born) is Aries, at 23°58'. The rising sign describes how you come across, the body you live in, and the way you start things. With Aries rising, the outward temperament is direct, competitive and quick to start — a pioneer's bearing that would rather act and correct than wait and be certain. Physical courage is real; patience is the lesson of the life.
     [2] The rising degree also falls in a nakshatra — one of the 27 star-groups the zodiac is divided into, each with its own character — called Bharani, in its 4th quarter. That nakshatra colours the body and the first impression you make: intense, transformative bearing; carries burdens and creative extremes.
     [3] Every sign has a ruling planet, and the ruler of your rising sign — Mars — is the planet that stands for you personally in the chart. It sits in your 9th house in Sagittarius, which is a sign that is neutral to it — neither helped nor hindered. Its overall strength (a combined score from its sign, house, aspects and other factors) comes to 50 out of 100 (moderate). Because it sits in the 9th, the centre of gravity of your life leans toward fortune: that is the agenda that colours everything you attempt, and your sense of who you are is bound up in it.
     [4] Mars itself falls in the nakshatra Mula, in its 1st quarter, whose ruling planet is Ketu. The flavour of that nakshatra is: root-cutting inquiry; destroys to find foundational truth. Ketu, the nakshatra's ruler, is neutral toward Mars, so this quality comes through plainly — neither boosted nor blocked.
     [5] For Aries rising, Jupiter is the most helpful planet, because it rules your 9th house of fortune; the Sun, ruling your 5th house of intelligence and children, supports it. Mars is on your side as the ruler of your rising sign, even though it also rules the difficult 8th. Saturn (your 10th and 11th houses), Mercury (3rd and 6th) and Venus (2nd and 7th — one of the two 'health-watch' houses) each need to be well placed before their periods deliver cleanly.
  == Your Moon — the mind and its moods
     [1] In Vedic astrology the Moon stands for the mind: your moods, your instincts, what you need in order to feel safe. Your Moon is in Sagittarius in your 9th house, which is an enemy's sign, where it meets resistance; its overall strength comes to 44 out of 100 (weak). With the Moon in Sagittarius, the mind is optimistic and philosophical and needs room; feeling attaches itself to meaning and to freedom, and confinement — physical or moral — is the real distress.
     [2] The Moon falls in the nakshatra Mula, in its 4th quarter, whose ruling planet is Ketu. The flavour of that nakshatra is: root-cutting inquiry; destroys to find foundational truth. This is the single most personal signature in your chart: it sets the sequence of your planetary periods (the Vimshottari dasha, the timing system used throughout this app), and it describes how you actually experience your own life from the inside. Ketu, the nakshatra's ruler, is neutral toward Moon, so this quality comes through plainly — neither boosted nor blocked.
     [3] Because the Moon sits in your 9th house, your emotional life revolves around fortune. That is where you go for comfort, and it is also where you are most easily unsettled.
  == Your Sun — the will and the core self
     [1] The Sun stands for your will, your sense of self, and the things you need to be respected for. Your Sun is in Capricorn in your 10th house, which is a sign that is neutral to it — neither helped nor hindered; its overall strength comes to 51 out of 100 (moderate). With the Sun in Capricorn, the will is disciplined and structural: your identity rests on responsibility carried and authority earned.
     [2] Because the Sun sits in your 10th house, your identity is staked on career. This is the arena in which you need to matter, and a setback here feels like a failure of self rather than of circumstance.
     [3] The Sun falls in the nakshatra Shravana, in its 1st quarter, whose ruling planet is Moon. The flavour of that nakshatra is: attentive listening; learning, fame and connective intelligence. Moon, the nakshatra's ruler, is a friend of Sun, so this quality comes through warmly and is easy for others to read.
  == Your strongest and weakest planets
     [1] Each planet in your chart has been scored for overall strength out of 100 — a combination of the sign it is in, the house, the aspects it receives, and a few other classical factors. Mercury is your strongest planet at 53 (moderate), sitting in your 9th house in Sagittarius. Its qualities are the ones you reach for by default and perform best with under pressure — whatever Mercury stands for is where you are genuinely, reliably competent. The biggest things in its favour are: House placement (9); Aspect from Jupiter; Nakshatra lord Venus (Purva Ashadha) — friend.
     [2] Jupiter is your weakest at 15 (afflicted), in your 3rd house in Gemini. This is the chart's soft flank: whatever Jupiter stands for is where you tend to over-compensate, avoid, or quietly hand off to other people. The biggest things weighing on it are: Dignity: Great Enemy's Sign (Adhi Shatru); Aspect from Moon; Aspect from Mars.
     [3] A lot of your personality is the negotiation between these two. Growth, for this chart, looks less like adding to Mercury — which needs no help — and more like refusing to let Jupiter's weakness set the terms, because that is precisely the area you are most tempted to route around.
  == Atmakaraka — what this life is for
     [1] Saturn is your Atmakaraka — literally the 'soul significator', the planet that has travelled furthest through its sign (24°37'), which the Jaimini school of astrology treats as the planet carrying the deepest purpose of the life. In simple terms: whatever Saturn stands for — placed in your 9th house in Sagittarius — is the lesson this life keeps returning to, in relationships, in work, everywhere. It is less "what you do" and more "what you cannot avoid becoming good at".
     [2] Its Karakamsa — the sign the Atmakaraka occupies in the Navamsa, the ninth-division chart — is Scorpio: the inner room where that lesson is actually worked out. A Scorpio Karakamsa colours the soul's work as intense, private and penetrating, with feeling that runs deep and is rarely displayed. Loyalty is absolute and so is its withdrawal — this temperament does not do half-measures.
  == Navamsa — the inner person
     [1] The Navamsa (D-9) is a second chart derived from your birth chart by dividing each sign into nine parts. Tradition reads it for the inner person and for marriage, and treats it as the test of whether what the birth chart promises actually holds. Its rising sign is Scorpio. If the birth chart is how life presents itself, the Navamsa is how you are on the inside once the noise settles — and a Scorpio inner nature is intense, private and penetrating, with feeling that runs deep and is rarely displayed. Loyalty is absolute and so is its withdrawal — this temperament does not do half-measures. When the outer sign (Aries) and inner sign (Scorpio) differ in element, people who only know you casually often misread you; those close to you meet the Navamsa.
     [2] Venus is 'Vargottama' — in the same sign in both the birth chart and the Navamsa. A Vargottama planet keeps its promises: what it shows on the surface is what it actually is underneath, and it holds steady under pressure.
  == By the classical six-fold measure (Shadbala)
     [1] Shadbala is the full classical strength calculation — six separate measures (position, direction, time of birth, motion, nature and aspects) added together and expressed in units called rupas, with each planet having a minimum it is expected to reach. By that measure Sun is your strongest planet at 9.57 rupas (147% of its required minimum) and Moon the weakest at 5.08 rupas (85%). Note this differs from the quicker overall score above — the six-fold measure weighs birth-time and motion factors the quick score does not; where they disagree, the Shadbala verdict is the classical one.
     [2] Practically: lean on Sun's themes when the stakes are high, and give Moon's themes more preparation time than feels necessary.
  == How you are seen vs how you are (Arudha Lagna)
     [1] The Arudha Lagna is the point in the chart that stands for your public image — how the world perceives you, as distinct from how you actually are. Yours falls in Leo, while your actual rising sign is Aries. So the public image runs on Leo's wavelength — warm, proud and naturally centre-stage, where dignity is not vanity but a working requirement — while the person underneath operates as Aries. The gap is not dishonesty; it is simply that your reputation forms around the themes of your 5th house. Knowing which conversations are about the image and which are about you is a lifelong advantage.
  == Yoga signatures in the personality
     [1] A 'yoga' is a named combination of planets that the classical texts single out as producing a specific result. 2 combinations in your chart stamp themselves directly on the character: Chandra-Mangala Yoga, Sunapha Yoga.
     [2] Chandra-Mangala Yoga — Moon and Mars conjoin — an earning combination: entrepreneurial drive fused with instinct. Wealth through self-effort, with a temper that needs channels, not suppression.
     [3] Sunapha Yoga — Venus occupies the sign after the Moon. Sunapha builds self-made standing: intelligence applied to earning, wealth accumulated by your own effort rather than inherited, and a reputation that grows on results. What Venus governs is where that earning tends to concentrate.
  == Which planets work for you, and which against
     [1] Which planets help you and which hinder you is not fixed — it depends on your rising sign, because the rising sign decides which houses each planet rules. A planet that rules good houses for Aries rising works in your favour whatever its natural reputation; one that rules difficult houses works against you even if it is a 'good' planet in general.
     [2] Working for you: Jupiter — in your 3rd house, in a great enemy's sign, strength 15 out of 100 (afflicted); Sun — in your 10th house, in a sign neutral to it, strength 51 out of 100 (moderate); Mars — in your 9th house, in a sign neutral to it, strength 50 out of 100 (moderate). The traits these planets govern are the ones you can lean on without second-guessing, and their main periods (dashas) feel like the personality working as designed.
     [3] Working against you: Saturn — in your 9th house, in an enemy's sign, strength 37 out of 100 (weak); Mercury — in your 9th house, in an enemy's sign, strength 53 out of 100 (moderate); Venus — in your 10th house, in a great friend's sign, strength 38 out of 100 (weak). These are not evil planets — they are the ones whose house rulerships pull against your rising sign's interests. Where one of them is strong, you have a talent that repeatedly gets you into trouble; where weak, a blind spot you rarely notice.
     [4] Neutral: Moon — in your 9th house, in an enemy's sign, strength 44 out of 100 (weak). These take their colour from whatever they sit with rather than carrying an agenda of their own.

--- houses ---
  == 1st House — Aries  occupied=false lord=Ma planets=[] aspects=[]
     [1] Your 1st house falls in Aries. This is the part of the chart that covers self, body, vitality, temperament and the overall direction of life. No planet sits in it. That is not a weakness in itself — most houses in most charts are empty. An empty house is read the way the classical texts prescribe: by the condition of the planet that rules it, and by the planets that aspect it from elsewhere.
     [2] Aries is ruled by Mars, so Mars is the ruler of your 1st house — the planet whose condition decides how this area of life goes. Mars sits in your 9th house at 2°28' Sagittarius, which is a sign that is neutral to it — neither helped nor hindered. Counting from the 1st house itself, Mars stands 9 places along — the 9th from its own house, which is one of the supportive positions (the corner houses 1, 4, 7, 10 and the fortunate houses 5 and 9). The ruler actively backs what it rules. Because the ruler sits in the 9th, the affairs of your 1st house get worked out mostly through fortune — that is where they actually play out in daily life. Its overall strength — a combined score from its sign, house, aspects and other factors — comes to 50 out of 100 (moderate). Mars can carry the 1st house, but results here follow your own effort closely rather than being handed to you.
     [3] The ruler, Mars, also falls in the star-group (nakshatra) called Mula, in its 1st quarter. Each nakshatra has its own ruling planet and its own flavour — here the ruler is Ketu, and the flavour is: root-cutting inquiry; destroys to find foundational truth. Ketu, the ruler of that nakshatra, is neutral toward Mars, so this flavour comes through as it is — neither boosted nor blocked.
     [4] No planet aspects the 1st house. Its affairs run on the ruler's condition alone, with nothing from outside either reinforcing or interfering — outcomes here are quieter and more in your own hands than elsewhere in the chart.
     [5] In short: with no planet in it to speak for it, how your 1st house goes rests on Mars's condition in the 9th and on the aspects described above. Anything that strengthens Mars — its main periods, its transits, deliberate attention to what it stands for — improves this house; nothing else reaches it directly.
  == 2nd House — Taurus  occupied=false lord=Ve planets=[] aspects=[Ra:5]
     [1] Your 2nd house falls in Taurus. This is the part of the chart that covers wealth, family, speech, food and accumulated resources. No planet sits in it. That is not a weakness in itself — most houses in most charts are empty. An empty house is read the way the classical texts prescribe: by the condition of the planet that rules it, and by the planets that aspect it from elsewhere.
     [2] Taurus is ruled by Venus, so Venus is the ruler of your 2nd house — the planet whose condition decides how this area of life goes. Venus sits in your 10th house at 1°42' Capricorn, which is the sign of a great friend, where it is warmly hosted. It is also retrograde (appearing to move backwards in the sky at your birth). Counting from the 2nd house itself, Venus stands 9 places along — the 9th from its own house, which is one of the supportive positions (the corner houses 1, 4, 7, 10 and the fortunate houses 5 and 9). The ruler actively backs what it rules. Because the ruler sits in the 10th, the affairs of your 2nd house get worked out mostly through career — that is where they actually play out in daily life. Its overall strength — a combined score from its sign, house, aspects and other factors — comes to 38 out of 100 (weak), which is low. The 2nd house is under-resourced at its ruler, and its affairs need conscious support from you before they hold weight.
     [3] The ruler, Venus, also falls in the star-group (nakshatra) called Uttara Ashadha, in its 2nd quarter. Each nakshatra has its own ruling planet and its own flavour — here the ruler is Sun, and the flavour is: later, lasting victory; patient universal ambition. Sun, the ruler of that nakshatra, is a natural enemy of Venus, so even a well-placed Venus has its results filtered through an unsympathetic hand — expect what it promises to arrive altered, later, or at a price.
     [4] Rahu aspects this house — that is, it reaches into it from where it sits, the 10th house in Capricorn, in a great friend's sign, retrograde. By nature Rahu is a hard planet (a 'malefic'), and for your rising sign it is neither for you nor against you by rulership (functionally neutral). The aspect landing here is Rahu's special 5th-house aspect — an amplifier of intelligence and of desire. Rahu's aspect on the 2nd creates hunger around wealth, family and speech — accumulation is driven, sometimes through unorthodox or foreign channels, and the voice can persuade past the facts. Family history carries something unresolved. A hard planet in a strong position does not merely trouble a house — it also forces you to become competent in its affairs, charging the discipline as its price.
     [5] Every aspect reaching this house comes from a hard planet. With no gentle planet to balance them, what this house stands for needs conscious defending, and the house tends to mature through difficulty rather than ease — which also means it usually matures well.
     [6] In short: with no planet in it to speak for it, how your 2nd house goes rests on Venus's condition in the 10th and on the aspects described above. Anything that strengthens Venus — its main periods, its transits, deliberate attention to what it stands for — improves this house; nothing else reaches it directly.

     [3] The ruler, Mercury, also falls in the star-group (nakshatra) called Purva Ashadha, in its 2nd quarter.      [4] Jupiter sits here at 8°42' Gemini. Jupiter in the 3rd gives wise initiative — courage tempered by ethics. Siblings prosper or are themselves guru-figures. Writing and teaching flow naturally, though ambition here is softer than talent; effort must be deliberate. Gemini belongs to a great enemy of Jupiter, so it struggles to be itself here. Expect what Jupiter stands for to be taxed — and expect deliberate attention to this planet to pay back more than usual, precisely because it starts from behind. Jupiter is retrograde — it appeared to be moving backwards through the sky when you were born. In practice that turns its energy inward and intensifies it: the themes of this house come back around more than once, and matters tend to conclude on the second or third pass rather than the first.
     [5] Jupiter falls in the nakshatra Ardra (1st quarter), ruled by Rahu, whose flavour is: stormy, penetrating intellect; breakthroughs after inner turbulence. Rahu, the ruler of that nakshatra, is neutral toward Jupiter, so this flavour comes through as it is — neither boosted nor blocked.
     [6] Moon aspects this house — that is, it reaches into it from where it sits, the 9th house in Sagittarius, in an enemy's sign. By nature Moon is a hard planet (a 'malefic'), and for your rising sign it is neither for you nor against you by rulership (functionally neutral). The aspect landing here is the full 7th-house aspect every planet casts. Moonlight on the 3rd makes communication warm and instinctive and courage emotional rather than calculated; you act when moved. Siblings are close, and short journeys are undertaken on impulse. A hard planet in a weak position troubles the house without building much capability in return: the friction comes with little compensating discipline.
     [7] Mars also aspects this house, from the 9th house in Sagittarius, in a sign neutral to it — a hard planet by nature, and one that works in your favour by the houses it rules. The aspect landing here is the full 7th-house aspect every planet casts — in Mars's case direct and unavoidable. Mars on the 3rd is one of its best glances: raw courage, technical skill, physical stamina, and self-effort that actually converts into results. Rivalry with siblings is likely and, handled well, formative. Coming from a hard planet in an ordinary condition, the aspect keeps this house honest — pressure you learn to work with rather than damage. Its rulerships soften this considerably — a hard planet that works for your rising sign behaves far better than its reputation.
     [8] Mercury also aspects this house, from the 9th house in Sagittarius, in an enemy's sign — a hard planet by nature, and one that pulls against your interests by the houses it rules. The aspect landing here is the full 7th-house aspect every planet casts. Mercury on the 3rd doubles its own ground — skills, writing, media, short journeys and dexterous hands. A hard planet in a weak position troubles the house without building much capability in return: the friction comes with little compensating discipline.
     [9] Saturn also aspects this house, from the 9th house in Sagittarius, in an enemy's sign — a hard planet by nature, and one that pulls against your interests by the houses it rules. Saturn's glance on the 3rd converts courage into endurance: you outlast rather than outcharge. Because the ruler sits in the 9th, the affairs of your 4th house get worked out mostly through fortune — that is where they actually play out in daily life. Its overall strength — a combined score from its sign, house, aspects and other factors — comes to 44 out of 100 (weak), which is low.      [4] Ketu sits here at 23°30' Cancer. Ketu rules no sign of its own. It works through the planet that owns the sign it sits in, through the ruler of its star-group (nakshatra), and through whatever it sits beside — a shadow that takes on the colour of its surroundings rather than a house ruler with an agenda of its own. Ketu in the 4th unroots the home: moves, a mother-bond marked by absence or spirituality, comfort found nowhere until found within. Property matters stay unglamorous. The true homeland you seek has no postal code. Cancer belongs to a great enemy of Ketu, so it struggles to be itself here. Expect what Ketu stands for to be taxed — and expect deliberate attention to this planet to pay back more than usual, precisely because it starts from behind. Ketu is retrograde — it appeared to be moving backwards through the sky when you were born. In practice that turns its energy inward and intensifies it: the themes of this house come back around more than once, and matters tend to conclude on the second or third pass rather than the first.
     [5] Ketu falls in the nakshatra Ashlesha (3rd quarter), ruled by Mercury, whose flavour is: coiled, hypnotic insight; strategy, secrecy and kundalini intensity. Mercury, the ruler of that nakshatra, is neutral toward Ketu, so this flavour comes through as it is — neither boosted nor blocked.
     [6] Sun aspects this house — that is, it reaches into it from where it sits, the 10th house in Capricorn, in a sign neutral to it. The aspect landing here is the full 7th-house aspect every planet casts. The Sun's glance on the 4th brings prestige to the home and property held in your own name, but it heats the domestic atmosphere: there is an authority in the house and a certain absence of softness. What the chart says about your mother carries the father's or the state's imprint. Coming from a hard planet in an ordinary condition, the aspect keeps this house honest — pressure you learn to work with rather than damage. Its rulerships soften this considerably — a hard planet that works for your rising sign behaves far better than its reputation.
     [7] Mars also aspects this house, from the 9th house in Sagittarius, in a sign neutral to it — a hard planet by nature, and one that works in your favour by the houses it rules. The aspect landing here is Mars's special 8th-house aspect — the sudden, wounding one that arrives without notice. Mars's aspect on the 4th disturbs the home's peace — a heated domestic atmosphere, property acquired or held through dispute, and a restlessness that resists settling. It does give real capacity with land, machinery and construction. Coming from a hard planet in an ordinary condition, the aspect keeps this house honest — pressure you learn to work with rather than damage. Its rulerships soften this considerably — a hard planet that works for your rising sign behaves far better than its reputation.
     [8] Venus also aspects this house, from the 10th house in Capricorn, in a great friend's sign, retrograde — a gentle planet by nature, and one that pulls against your interests by the houses it rules. A hard planet in a strong position does not merely trouble a house — it also forces you to become competent in its affairs, charging the discipline as its price.

Saturn in the 9th tests faith before granting it: a dutiful or distant father, dharma adopted only after doubt has done its full audit. Fortune is real but late-arriving and labour-priced. Traditional disciplines reward deeply. Sagittarius belongs to an enemy of Saturn, so it meets resistance here — a guest whose host does not much like it. Results in this house take roughly twice the usual persistence to arrive.
     [11] Saturn falls in the nakshatra Purva Ashadha (4th quarter), ruled by Venus, whose flavour is: invincible early victories; proud, philosophical, water-blessed. Venus, the ruler of that nakshatra, is a natural friend of Saturn, so this flavour is passed on willingly and arrives in a form you recognise.
     [12] Moon and Mars both sit in nakshatras ruled by Ketu, which tightens their pairing: they do not merely share a sign, they answer to the same planet, and Ketu's main periods bring both to the surface at once.

     [14] Jupiter aspects this house — that is, it reaches into it from where it sits, the 3rd house in Gemini, in a great enemy's sign, retrograde.      [16] Moon–Mars (Chandra-Mangala) monetises instinct: entrepreneurial reflexes, protective passion, wealth built through decisive emotional intelligence.   == 10th House — Capricorn  occupied=true lord=Sa planets=[Su,Ve,Ra] aspects=[Ke:7]
     [1] Your 10th house falls in Capricorn. This is the part of the chart that covers career, status, authority, karma and public achievement. Sun, Venus, Rahu all sit in it, which makes this one of the main arenas of your life — a lot happens here, and it happens to you directly.
     [2] Capricorn is ruled by Saturn, so Saturn is the ruler of your 10th house — the planet whose condition decides how this area of life goes. Saturn sits in your 9th house at 24°37' Sagittarius, which is an enemy's sign, where it meets resistance. Counting from the 10th house itself, Saturn stands 12 places along — the 12th from its own house, which is one of the three difficult positions (the 6th, 8th and 12th, called 'dusthana'). The matters of this house are undermined a little from within, and putting them right takes deliberate, sustained effort rather than luck. Because the ruler sits in the 9th, the affairs of your 10th house get worked out mostly through fortune — that is where they actually play out in daily life. Its overall strength — a combined score from its sign, house, aspects and other factors — comes to 37 out of 100 (weak), which is low. The 10th house is under-resourced at its ruler, and its affairs need conscious support from you before they hold weight.
     [3] The ruler, Saturn, also falls in the star-group (nakshatra) called Purva Ashadha, in its 4th quarter. Each nakshatra has its own ruling planet and its own flavour — here the ruler is Venus, and the flavour is: invincible early victories; proud, philosophical, water-blessed. Venus, the ruler of that nakshatra, is a natural friend of Saturn, so this flavour is passed on willingly and arrives in a form you recognise.
     [4] Sun sits here at 10°18' Capricorn. For your Aries rising sign, Sun rules your 5th house — intelligence — which makes it a planet that works in your favour by the houses it rules (a 'functional benefic'). Sun in the 10th is the Sun at full directional strength ('dig bala' — each planet has one house where it is naturally strongest, and the 10th is the Sun's). Career is destiny here: visibility, command and recognition from authority. You rise in hierarchies almost by gravity, though the cost is often paid at home. Capricorn is neutral ground for Sun — the sign neither helps nor hinders it. What you get out of this house tracks what you put in almost exactly, with no subsidy and no surcharge.
     [5] Sun falls in the nakshatra Shravana (1st quarter), ruled by Moon, whose flavour is: attentive listening; learning, fame and connective intelligence. Moon, the ruler of that nakshatra, is a natural friend of Sun, so this flavour is passed on willingly and arrives in a form you recognise.
     [6] Venus sits here at 1°42' Capricorn. For your Aries rising sign, Venus rules your 2nd and 7th houses — wealth and marriage — which makes it a planet whose house rulerships pull against your interests, whatever its natural character (a 'functional malefic'). It also rules one of the two houses (the 2nd and 7th) that the tradition watches for health in its main periods, so its periods deserve a little extra care. Venus in the 10th makes career of charm and craft: design, entertainment, diplomacy, luxury commerce, public relations. Reputation is likeable — a real asset and a subtle trap, since being liked can be preferred over being right. Capricorn belongs to a great friend of Venus, so it is warmly hosted here. Its promises are delivered with goodwill, and usually with a little extra. Venus is retrograde — it appeared to be moving backwards through the sky when you were born. In practice that turns its energy inward and intensifies it: the themes of this house come back around more than once, and matters tend to conclude on the second or third pass rather than the first.
     [7] Venus falls in the nakshatra Uttara Ashadha (2nd quarter), ruled by Sun, whose flavour is: later, lasting victory; patient universal ambition. Sun, the ruler of that nakshatra, is a natural enemy of Venus, so even a well-placed Venus has its results filtered through an unsympathetic hand — expect what it promises to arrive altered, later, or at a price.
     [8] Rahu sits here at 23°30' Capricorn. Rahu rules no sign of its own. It works through the planet that owns the sign it sits in, through the ruler of its star-group (nakshatra), and through whatever it sits beside — a shadow that takes on the colour of its surroundings rather than a house ruler with an agenda of its own. Rahu in the 10th is the fame algorithm: obsessive career ambition, mass-market instincts, success through technology, media or foreign systems. Reputation inflates fast — and needs ethical ballast to avoid equally fast correction. Capricorn belongs to a great friend of Rahu, so it is warmly hosted here. Its promises are delivered with goodwill, and usually with a little extra. Rahu is retrograde — it appeared to be moving backwards through the sky when you were born. In practice that turns its energy inward and intensifies it: the themes of this house come back around more than once, and matters tend to conclude on the second or third pass rather than the first.
     [9] Rahu falls in the nakshatra Dhanishta (1st quarter), ruled by Mars, whose flavour is: abundant rhythm; music, wealth and adaptive movement. Mars, the ruler of that nakshatra, is a natural enemy of Rahu, so even a well-placed Rahu has its results filtered through an unsympathetic hand — expect what it promises to arrive altered, later, or at a price.
     [10] Ketu aspects this house — that is, it reaches into it from where it sits, the 4th house in Cancer, in a great enemy's sign, retrograde. By nature Ketu is a hard planet (a 'malefic'), and for your rising sign it is neither for you nor against you by rulership (functionally neutral). The aspect landing here is the full 7th-house aspect every planet casts — in Ketu's case direct detachment. Ketu on the 10th gives a career pursued without hunger for its rewards, often technical, healing or spiritual, and a public standing you are oddly indifferent to. Ambition arrives late, if at all, and the work is better for it. A hard planet in a weak position troubles the house without building much capability in return: the friction comes with little compensating discipline.
     [11] Every aspect reaching this house comes from a hard planet. With no gentle planet to balance them, what this house stands for needs conscious defending, and the house tends to mature through difficulty rather than ease — which also means it usually matures well.
     [12] Sun–Venus pairs sovereign and artist — creative authority, refined presence, romance complicated by pride. Venus close to combustion strains what the chart promises about marriage; the remedy is letting the partner shine without auditing the wattage. The two planets are 8.6° apart and their combined strength is only 45 out of 100, so they share a house more than a purpose. The promise is real but muted, and tends to surface mainly during Sun's main periods.
     [13] Sun–Rahu eclipses the ego periodically: outsized ambition for recognition shadowed by crises of legitimacy. There is static around the father. Matured, it gives unconventional authority that rewrites the org chart it was denied a place on. The two planets are 13.2° apart and their combined strength is only 45 out of 100, so they share a house more than a purpose. The promise is real but muted, and tends to surface mainly during Sun's main periods.
     [14] Venus–Rahu glamorises appetite: cinematic romance, luxury cravings, magnetic public appeal. The entertainment and beauty industries run on this fuel. Discernment in love is the tax; unpaid, the sweetness ferments. Their combined strength is just 34 out of 100 across 21.8°, so this combination is compromised — you will feel its difficulties more reliably than its gifts. Deliberately strengthening Rahu is the single most useful thing to do about it.
     [15] With 3 planets sharing one house, it works like a committee: Sun chairs it as the strongest member, the group spans 21.8° and carries a combined strength of 36 out of 100 (afflicted). The crowding is a liability rather than an asset here — the house is overloaded, and what it stands for needs deliberate, sustained repair.
  == 11th House — Aquarius  occupied=false lord=Sa planets=[] aspects=[Ju:9,Sa:3]
     [1] Your 11th house falls in Aquarius. This is the part of the chart that covers gains, income, networks, elder siblings and fulfilment of desires. No planet sits in it. That is not a weakness in itself — most houses in most charts are empty. An empty house is read the way the classical texts prescribe: by the condition of the planet that rules it, and by the planets that aspect it from elsewhere.
     [2] Aquarius is ruled by Saturn, so Saturn is the ruler of your 11th house — the planet whose condition decides how this area of life goes. Saturn sits in your 9th house at 24°37' Sagittarius, which is an enemy's sign, where it meets resistance. Counting from the 11th house itself, Saturn stands 11 places along — the 11th from its own house, a workable but unremarkable position that neither helps nor hurts. Because the ruler sits in the 9th, the affairs of your 11th house get worked out mostly through fortune — that is where they actually play out in daily life. Its overall strength — a combined score from its sign, house, aspects and other factors — comes to 37 out of 100 (weak), which is low. The 11th house is under-resourced at its ruler, and its affairs need conscious support from you before they hold weight.
     [3] The ruler, Saturn, also falls in the star-group (nakshatra) called Purva Ashadha, in its 4th quarter. Each nakshatra has its own ruling planet and its own flavour — here the ruler is Venus, and the flavour is: invincible early victories; proud, philosophical, water-blessed. Venus, the ruler of that nakshatra, is a natural friend of Saturn, so this flavour is passed on willingly and arrives in a form you recognise.
     [4] Jupiter aspects this house — that is, it reaches into it from where it sits, the 3rd house in Gemini, in a great enemy's sign, retrograde. By nature Jupiter is a gentle planet (a 'benefic'), and for your rising sign it is a planet that works in your favour by the houses it rules (a 'functional benefic'). The aspect landing here is Jupiter's special 9th-house aspect — its blessing on fortune and right living, and the most benevolent aspect there is. Jupiter's glance on the 11th expands gains and fills the network with people of standing; income grows steadily and desires are largely fulfilled. The risk is a portfolio inflated by optimism. The good intent is there, but the planet itself is poorly placed, so the protection is real yet thin, and tends to arrive later than you expect.
     [5] Saturn also aspects this house, from the 9th house in Sagittarius, in an enemy's sign — a hard planet by nature, and one that pulls against your interests by the houses it rules. The aspect landing here is Saturn's special 3rd-house aspect — the grinding one, which wears down rather than blocks outright. Saturn's aspect on the 11th makes gains slow but cumulative and the network older, professional and useful — income from labour, systems, property or long-held positions. Elder siblings carry weight or distance. A hard planet in a weak position troubles the house without building much capability in return: the friction comes with little compensating discipline.
     [6] The house receives 1 gentle and 1 hard aspect — a contested area whose results swing depending on which of these planets is running its main period (dasha) at the time.
     [7] In short: with no planet in it to speak for it, how your 11th house goes rests on Saturn's condition in the 9th and on the aspects described above. Anything that strengthens Saturn — its main periods, its transits, deliberate attention to what it stands for — improves this house; nothing else reaches it directly.
  == 12th House — Pisces  occupied=false lord=Ju planets=[] aspects=[Ma:4,Ke:9]
     [1] Your 12th house falls in Pisces. This is the part of the chart that covers expenditure, losses, foreign lands, isolation, sleep and moksha. No planet sits in it. That is not a weakness in itself — most houses in most charts are empty. An empty house is read the way the classical texts prescribe: by the condition of the planet that rules it, and by the planets that aspect it from elsewhere.
     [2] Pisces is ruled by Jupiter, so Jupiter is the ruler of your 12th house — the planet whose condition decides how this area of life goes. Jupiter sits in your 3rd house at 8°42' Gemini, which is the sign of a great enemy, where it struggles to be itself. It is also retrograde (appearing to move backwards in the sky at your birth). Counting from the 12th house itself, Jupiter stands 4 places along — the 4th from its own house, which is one of the supportive positions (the corner houses 1, 4, 7, 10 and the fortunate houses 5 and 9). The ruler actively backs what it rules. Because the ruler sits in the 3rd, the affairs of your 12th house get worked out mostly through courage — that is where they actually play out in daily life. Its overall strength — a combined score from its sign, house, aspects and other factors — comes to 15 out of 100 (afflicted), which is low. The 12th house is under-resourced at its ruler, and its affairs need conscious support from you before they hold weight.
     [3] The ruler, Jupiter, also falls in the star-group (nakshatra) called Ardra, in its 1st quarter. Each nakshatra has its own ruling planet and its own flavour — here the ruler is Rahu, and the flavour is: stormy, penetrating intellect; breakthroughs after inner turbulence. Rahu, the ruler of that nakshatra, is neutral toward Jupiter, so this flavour comes through as it is — neither boosted nor blocked.
     [4] Mars aspects this house — that is, it reaches into it from where it sits, the 9th house in Sagittarius, in a sign neutral to it. By nature Mars is a hard planet (a 'malefic'), and for your rising sign it is a planet that works in your favour by the houses it rules (a 'functional benefic'). The aspect landing here is Mars's special 4th-house aspect — Mars at close range, and the most disruptive of its three aspects. Mars's gaze on the 12th burns resources in hidden places: expenditure on disputes, secret enemies, or exertion abroad. Well directed, it is the energy of the person who works hard where no one is watching; poorly directed, it is sleep lost to unfinished fights. Coming from a hard planet in an ordinary condition, the aspect keeps this house honest — pressure you learn to work with rather than damage. Its rulerships soften this considerably — a hard planet that works for your rising sign behaves far better than its reputation.
     [5] Ketu also aspects this house, from the 4th house in Cancer, in a great enemy's sign, retrograde — a hard planet by nature, and neutral by rulership. The aspect landing here is Ketu's special 9th-house aspect — a refining influence on fortune and right living. Ketu's glance on the 12th is its most natural station: expenditure that quietly serves liberation, ease in solitude and in foreign lands, a vivid inner life in sleep. Among all the aspects, this is the strongest single indicator of moksha-orientation. A hard planet in a weak position troubles the house without building much capability in return: the friction comes with little compensating discipline.
     [6] Every aspect reaching this house comes from a hard planet. With no gentle planet to balance them, what this house stands for needs conscious defending, and the house tends to mature through difficulty rather than ease — which also means it usually matures well.
     [7] In short: with no planet in it to speak for it, how your 12th house goes rests on Jupiter's condition in the 3rd and on the aspects described above. Anything that strengthens Jupiter — its main periods, its transits, deliberate attention to what it stands for — improves this house; nothing else reaches it directly.

  == Job or business?
     [1] Job and business sit almost level in your chart, which is a real result rather than a fence-sit. A hybrid tends to work best: salaried work that quietly builds toward your own practice, or a business held steady by one anchor client. The thing to avoid is jumping between the two every few years without letting either compound.
     why(3) Service side: Mercury rules your 6th house and scores 53/100 for strength
     why(-12) Business side: Venus rules your 7th house and scores 38/100 for strength
  == The conditions you work best in
     [1] You need visible responsibility. A flat, anonymous role chafes on you within a year or two, however good the pay is — the thing that keeps you engaged is having your name attached to the outcome, including when it goes wrong.
     [2] Your 10th house falls in Capricorn, and work cultures with that sign's temperament are where you settle in fastest. It is worth weighing that against salary when you choose between two offers — the culture is the thing you live in every day.
     [3] Your Amatyakaraka sits in the 9th house, which is where career support tends to reach you from. Practically: the doors that open for you usually open through 9th-house channels rather than through cold applications.

That is worth knowing in advance, because it makes a lean year legible instead of frightening.

--- section: marriage ---
  headline: Your marriage indications are mixed: warm significators on one side, some classical delay markers on the other. For a chart like this the windows matter far more than the average does.
  score=55 verdict=none confidence=75
  == The marriage houses and significators
     [1] Marriage is read from your 7th house and from the planets that naturally stand for partnership. Below are the two deeper views the tradition adds to that: the marriage pada, which describes the institution, and the Navamsa, which describes the daily life inside it.
     [2] Your Upapada Lagna (the marriage pada — the arudha of the 12th house) falls in Gemini, and Mercury rules it, sitting in your 9th house, in an enemy's sign. This point describes marriage as a standing institution in your life rather than as romance — the household, the in-laws, the public fact of being married. Its condition is the best single indicator of how settled and respected that arrangement tends to feel from the inside.
     [3] Your Navamsa (the D-9 divisional chart of marriage and inner strength) is where married life is actually read, and it says this: the Navamsa rising sign is Scorpio, ruled by Mars; its 7th house falls in Taurus, with nothing in it; and Venus lands in Capricorn, Vargottama (the same sign in both D-1 and D-9 — a mark of stability), which means what it promises tends to hold. Treat this as the private texture of the marriage — what the two of you are like at home on an ordinary Tuesday, rather than what the relationship looks like to everyone else.
     why(-1) Your 7th house is ruled by Venus, and it sits in your 10th house, in a great friend's sign, with an overall strength of 38 out of 100. That is where your partnership life gets carried out — 10th-house matters and your marriage tend to move together.
     why(-1) Venus is your natural significator for marriage, and it sits in your 10th house, in a great friend's sign (38/100). A significator in good condition shows up as ease in being close to someone: affection you can express, and a partnership that gets easier rather than heavier with time. [BPHS, kalatra karaka]
     why(2) Venus is your Darakaraka (the spouse significator — least advanced by degree), and it sits in your 10th house. The person you marry tends to carry that planet's signature — its temperament, its pace, its way of handling difficulty — closely enough that the description usually feels familiar once you meet them. [Jaimini Upadesa Sutras, chara karakas]
  == Mangal Dosha
     [1] Mars stands in the 1st from your Moon, and the 12th from Venus, which technically forms Mangal Dosha — but your chart cancels it: Jupiter aspects Mars — the classical benefic override. That means the heat is there without the affliction the tradition warns about. In daily life it reads as directness rather than damage: you say the difficult thing early, which is usually kinder than the alternative.
  == What your partner tends to be like
     [1] Because Venus rules your 7th house, the person you marry tends to carry that planet's nature — you are drawn to it, and you recognise it quickly. Tradition also reads a direction from this, South-East of your birthplace, but hold that one loosely: direction rules are the softest technique on this page.
     [2] There are no strong foreign markers on your marriage axis, which leans the reading toward a partner from a familiar cultural circle. That is a tendency in the chart, not a boundary on your life — plenty of these charts marry across every kind of distance.
     [3] Venus, as your spouse significator, colours their temperament more than anything else here. Look for a strong Venus signature in whom you actually choose — it is usually more visible in how they behave under stress than in how they present at first.
  == What tradition offers, and what actually helps
     [1] The standard literature recommends strengthening Venus in the traditional way — Friday observances, white clothing, courtesy toward your partner. The last of those is the one that carries the weight: attention paid to the relationship is what the practice is really training.

  == Three different lives abroad, scored separately
     [1] Travelling often, living abroad for years, and settling permanently are three different things, and a chart can be strong for one and quiet on the others. They are scored independently here for that reason.
     [2] These are tendencies rather than certainties. A strong score means the chart supports that shape of life and it tends to come easily; a low one means it costs more effort, not that it is closed to you.
     [3] Open any row to see what moved its number. A ▲ is a signature that pulls you abroad, a ▼ is something that holds you where you are. A low score with only ▲ reasons under it simply means few of the classical signatures are present — nothing in the chart is pushing back.

  == Direction — the softest reading here

--- section: cautions ---
  headline: 10 areas in your chart deserve conscious handling rather than luck. Each one below comes with something practical to do about it, and none of them is a verdict.

  == How to read this
     [1] These are tendencies, not sentences, and none of them is a verdict on your life. Classical astrology lists them for one reason: so you can steer. That is why every caution below arrives with the thing that actually helps — a caution without a counter-measure is just anxiety with a citation.
     [2] Nothing here is medical, legal or financial advice, and none of it should stand in for a professional who can look at your actual situation.

  == Where these come from
     [1] From your chart: the ruler of your rising sign (Mars), the ruler of your Moon's nakshatra (Ketu) and your strongest supportive planet (Sun) are the ones working in your favour. Their numbers, colours, days and directions are what this section calls yours.
     [2] From your birth date: Moolank 6 and Bhagyank 8, and a name number of 2 on the chaldean system, with a Kua number of 1 — each of which tradition assigns to a planet.
     [3] Treat all of this the way tradition actually intends it: as a set of preferences that tilt the odds slightly and cost nothing to follow, not as rules that decide outcomes. Wearing your colour to an interview is worth about as much as the confidence it gives you, which is not nothing.

     # The foundation: Aries Lagna
       Your Lagna is at 23°58' Aries in Bharani pada 4.
       No planet occupies or aspects the 1st house — its lord carries the whole of it.

       Mars is at 2°28' Sagittarius in Mula pada 1, in a neutral sign.

     # Saturn's cycles

       - Next Sade Sati: Dec 2043 – Feb 2052.
     # What to watch, and what helps
       - Saturn is your obstructor — for your rising sign that role falls to the 11th lord. What helps: Build slack into anything this planet touches: pad the timeline, avoid single points of failure, keep a fallback warm.
       - Mercury rules your 6th — one of the harder houses — and sits in your 9th, carrying that friction into an area that matters to you. What helps: Keep 9th-house matters clean on paper: written agreements, no informal loans, explicit boundaries with the people involved.
       - Saturn, Mercury, Venus — each rules houses that pull against your interests for an Aries rising chart, so its instincts tend to be the ones that get you into trouble. What helps: Give big commitments in these planets' areas one extra day and one outside opinion — that single habit removes most of what this placement costs people.
       - Venus rules both your 2nd and 7th houses, which classically makes it the planet whose main period is worth a little extra care about health and energy. What helps: During Venus's main period, keep routine check-ups current, insurance in force, and the basics — sleep, food, movement — non-negotiable.
       - Moon reaches only 85% of the strength the classical tables ask of it. What helps: Build a check into anything depending on Moon's themes — a review, a backup, a second quote.

It stamps the whole personality with formidable endurance and organisational authority built slowly and held for a long time. You govern systems, labour and institutions, and outlast flashier rivals. Coldness and a taste for control are the shadow.

      Saturn (an angle lord) and Mercury (a trine lord) conjoin. The classical engine of rank: when the ruler of a fortune house (1st, 5th or 9th) joins the ruler of a corner house (1st, 4th, 7th or 10th), status is lifted, and the results ripen in the main periods (dashas) of the two planets involved.

      Jupiter and Venus sit in each other's signs — a parivartana, or mutual exchange, linking the 2nd and 11th with the 4th and 9th. An exchange fuses two houses: neither can be read without the other, and whatever happens to one shows up in the other. Both lords own auspicious houses, which is the strong form of the exchange: the two areas actively fund each other, and your periods for either planet tend to move both at once.

--- personality ---
  headline: Aquarius rising, Aquarius Moon in Shatabhisha, Aquarius Sun — a personality led by Saturn and tested through Ketu.
  strongest=Sa weakest=Ke
  == Your rising sign — how you come across
     [1] Your rising sign (the 'Lagna' — the sign that was coming up over the eastern horizon at the moment you were born) is Aquarius, at 15°28'. The rising sign describes how you come across, the body you live in, and the way you start things. With Aquarius rising, the outward temperament is detached, systemic and unconventionally principled, thinking in groups and futures rather than individuals and moments. Independent to a fault, and warm in a way that surprises people.
     [2] The rising degree also falls in a nakshatra — one of the 27 star-groups the zodiac is divided into, each with its own character — called Shatabhisha, in its 3rd quarter. That nakshatra colours the body and the first impression you make: veiled healing; solitary depths, mysticism and medicine.
     [3] Every sign has a ruling planet, and the ruler of your rising sign — Saturn — is the planet that stands for you personally in the chart. It sits in your 1st house in Aquarius, which is its moolatrikona, the sign where it works most productively. Its overall strength (a combined score from its sign, house, aspects and other factors) comes to 69 out of 100 (strong). Because it sits in the 1st, the centre of gravity of your life leans toward self: that is the agenda that colours everything you attempt, and your sense of who you are is bound up in it.
     [4] Saturn itself falls in the nakshatra Shatabhisha, in its 2nd quarter, whose ruling planet is Rahu. The flavour of that nakshatra is: veiled healing; solitary depths, mysticism and medicine. Rahu, the nakshatra's ruler, is neutral toward Saturn, so this quality comes through plainly — neither boosted nor blocked.
     [5] For Aquarius rising, Venus is the yogakaraka — it rules your 4th house of home and your 9th house of fortune. Saturn is on your side as ruler of your rising sign (and of your 12th). Jupiter rules your 2nd and 11th, so it accumulates wealth but also carries the 2nd-house health-watch duty; the Moon (your 6th) and Mars (your 3rd and 10th) need to be well placed before their periods reward you.
  == Your Moon — the mind and its moods
     [1] In Vedic astrology the Moon stands for the mind: your moods, your instincts, what you need in order to feel safe. Your Moon is in Aquarius in your 1st house, which is an enemy's sign, where it meets resistance; its overall strength comes to 45 out of 100 (moderate). With the Moon in Aquarius, the mind is detached and observational, feeling at one remove and more comfortable with humanity than with individuals. Emotional independence is real, occasionally to the point of remoteness.
     [2] The Moon falls in the nakshatra Shatabhisha, in its 2nd quarter, whose ruling planet is Rahu. The flavour of that nakshatra is: veiled healing; solitary depths, mysticism and medicine. This is the single most personal signature in your chart: it sets the sequence of your planetary periods (the Vimshottari dasha, the timing system used throughout this app), and it describes how you actually experience your own life from the inside. Rahu, the nakshatra's ruler, is neutral toward Moon, so this quality comes through plainly — neither boosted nor blocked.
     [3] Because the Moon sits in your 1st house, your emotional life revolves around self. That is where you go for comfort, and it is also where you are most easily unsettled.
  == Your Sun — the will and the core self
     [1] The Sun stands for your will, your sense of self, and the things you need to be respected for. Your Sun is in Aquarius in your 1st house, which is the sign of a great enemy, where it struggles to be itself; its overall strength comes to 43 out of 100 (weak). With the Sun in Aquarius, the will is independent and reforming: your identity rests on principle rather than on approval.
     [2] Because the Sun sits in your 1st house, your identity is staked on self. This is the arena in which you need to matter, and a setback here feels like a failure of self rather than of circumstance.
     [3] The Sun falls in the nakshatra Purva Bhadrapada, in its 2nd quarter, whose ruling planet is Jupiter. The flavour of that nakshatra is: fiery twin-front transformation; intensity behind an austere face. Jupiter, the nakshatra's ruler, is a friend of Sun, so this quality comes through warmly and is easy for others to read.
  == Your strongest and weakest planets
     [1] Each planet in your chart has been scored for overall strength out of 100 — a combination of the sign it is in, the house, the aspects it receives, and a few other classical factors. Saturn is your strongest planet at 69 (strong), sitting in your 1st house in Aquarius. Its qualities are the ones you reach for by default and perform best with under pressure — whatever Saturn stands for is where you are genuinely, reliably competent. The biggest things in its favour are: Dignity: Moolatrikona; House placement (1); Aspect from Jupiter.
     [2] Ketu is your weakest at 12 (afflicted), in your 4th house in Taurus. This is the chart's soft flank: whatever Ketu stands for is where you tend to over-compensate, avoid, or quietly hand off to other people. The biggest things weighing on it are: Dignity: Debilitated (Neecha); Aspect from Mars; Aspect from Rahu.
     [3] A lot of your personality is the negotiation between these two. Growth, for this chart, looks less like adding to Saturn — which needs no help — and more like refusing to let Ketu's weakness set the terms, because that is precisely the area you are most tempted to route around.
  == Atmakaraka — what this life is for
     [1] Sun is your Atmakaraka — literally the 'soul significator', the planet that has travelled furthest through its sign (26°26'), which the Jaimini school of astrology treats as the planet carrying the deepest purpose of the life. In simple terms: whatever Sun stands for — placed in your 1st house in Aquarius — is the lesson this life keeps returning to, in relationships, in work, everywhere. It is less "what you do" and more "what you cannot avoid becoming good at".
     [2] Its Karakamsa — the sign the Atmakaraka occupies in the Navamsa, the ninth-division chart — is Taurus: the inner room where that lesson is actually worked out. A Taurus Karakamsa colours the soul's work as steady, sensual and immovable once settled, built for accumulation and endurance rather than speed. Comfort is sought sincerely and change resisted instinctively.
  == Navamsa — the inner person
     [1] The Navamsa (D-9) is a second chart derived from your birth chart by dividing each sign into nine parts. Tradition reads it for the inner person and for marriage, and treats it as the test of whether what the birth chart promises actually holds. Its rising sign is Aquarius. If the birth chart is how life presents itself, the Navamsa is how you are on the inside once the noise settles — and an Aquarius inner nature is detached, systemic and unconventionally principled, thinking in groups and futures rather than individuals and moments. Independent to a fault, and warm in a way that surprises people. When the outer sign (Aquarius) and inner sign (Aquarius) differ in element, people who only know you casually often misread you; those close to you meet the Navamsa.
  == By the classical six-fold measure (Shadbala)
     [1] Shadbala is the full classical strength calculation — six separate measures (position, direction, time of birth, motion, nature and aspects) added together and expressed in units called rupas, with each planet having a minimum it is expected to reach. By that measure Sun is your strongest planet at 9.16 rupas (141% of its required minimum) and Jupiter the weakest at 5.49 rupas (85%). Note this differs from the quicker overall score above — the six-fold measure weighs birth-time and motion factors the quick score does not; where they disagree, the Shadbala verdict is the classical one.
     [2] Practically: lean on Sun's themes when the stakes are high, and give Jupiter's themes more preparation time than feels necessary.
  == Aspects on your rising sign — who edits the personality
     [1] Planets influence houses they do not sit in by 'aspecting' them — casting their influence across the chart to particular houses. Every planet aspects the 7th house from itself, and Mars, Jupiter, Saturn, Rahu and Ketu each have extra special aspects. The following planets aspect your rising sign, and each one edits the personality others meet:
     [2] Jupiter aspects your rising sign from your 9th house — Jupiter's special 5th-house aspect — its blessing on intelligence, good counsel and children.
     [3] Each of these adds its own colour on top of the rising sign itself. If people describe you in ways that do not quite match the rising-sign portrait above, one of these planets is usually why.
  == How you are seen vs how you are (Arudha Lagna)
     [1] The Arudha Lagna is the point in the chart that stands for your public image — how the world perceives you, as distinct from how you actually are. Yours falls in Taurus, while your actual rising sign is Aquarius. So the public image runs on Taurus's wavelength — steady, sensual and immovable once settled, built for accumulation and endurance rather than speed — while the person underneath operates as Aquarius. The gap is not dishonesty; it is simply that your reputation forms around the themes of your 4th house. Knowing which conversations are about the image and which are about you is a lifelong advantage.
  == Yoga signatures in the personality
     [1] A 'yoga' is a named combination of planets that the classical texts single out as producing a specific result. 4 combinations in your chart stamp themselves directly on the character: Budhaditya Yoga, Chandra-Mangala Yoga, Sasa Yoga (Pancha Mahapurusha), Sunapha Yoga.
     [2] Budhaditya Yoga — Sun and Mercury conjoin in the 1st house, with Mercury clear of combustion: sharp administrative intellect and articulate authority.
     [3] Chandra-Mangala Yoga — Moon and Mars conjoin — an earning combination: entrepreneurial drive fused with instinct. Wealth through self-effort, with a temper that needs channels, not suppression.
     [4] Sasa Yoga (Pancha Mahapurusha) — Saturn is in its moolatrikona and occupies the 1st house, one of the four corner houses (a 'kendra') — one of the five Mahapurusha ("great person") combinations. It stamps the whole personality with formidable endurance and organisational authority built slowly and held for a long time. You govern systems, labour and institutions, and outlast flashier rivals. Coldness and a taste for control are the shadow.
     [5] Sunapha Yoga — Venus occupies the sign after the Moon. Sunapha builds self-made standing: intelligence applied to earning, wealth accumulated by your own effort rather than inherited, and a reputation that grows on results. What Venus governs is where that earning tends to concentrate.
     [6] Beneath that sits a resilience signature — Neechabhanga Raja Yoga — the combinations that convert this chart's difficulties into capability rather than damage. They rarely show early; they are what you are left holding after the hard decade.
     [7] Neechabhanga Raja Yoga — Rahu's debilitation is cancelled because its dispositor Mars occupies a kendra from Lagna; and its dispositor stands in a kendra from the Moon; and Ketu, exalted in that very sign's field, occupies a kendra. What begins as the chart's weakest point matures into a source of unusual strength, typically after early-life struggle in that portfolio.
     [8] Neechabhanga Raja Yoga — Ketu's debilitation is cancelled because Moon, exalted in that very sign's field, occupies a kendra; and its dispositor Venus is itself exalted; and the lord of its exaltation sign, Mars, occupies a kendra from Lagna. What begins as the chart's weakest point matures into a source of unusual strength, typically after early-life struggle in that portfolio.
  == Which planets work for you, and which against
     [1] Which planets help you and which hinder you is not fixed — it depends on your rising sign, because the rising sign decides which houses each planet rules. A planet that rules good houses for Aquarius rising works in your favour whatever its natural reputation; one that rules difficult houses works against you even if it is a 'good' planet in general.
     [2] Venus is the yogakaraka for Aquarius rising — the single most helpful planet your rising sign can have, because it rules one of the corner houses and one of the fortunate houses at the same time. Venus — in your 2nd house, where it is exalted, strength 61 out of 100 (strong). Its condition is the best single predictor of how far your personality converts into standing in the world.
     [3] Working for you: Venus — in your 2nd house, where it is exalted, strength 61 out of 100 (strong); Saturn — in your 1st house, in its moolatrikona sign, strength 69 out of 100 (strong). The traits these planets govern are the ones you can lean on without second-guessing, and their main periods (dashas) feel like the personality working as designed.
     [4] Working against you: Jupiter — in your 9th house, in a great enemy's sign, strength 45 out of 100 (moderate); Moon — in your 1st house, in an enemy's sign, strength 45 out of 100 (moderate); Mars — in your 1st house, in an enemy's sign, strength 46 out of 100 (moderate). These are not evil planets — they are the ones whose house rulerships pull against your rising sign's interests. Where one of them is strong, you have a talent that repeatedly gets you into trouble; where weak, a blind spot you rarely notice.
     [5] Neutral: Sun — in your 1st house, in a great enemy's sign, strength 43 out of 100 (weak); Mercury — in your 1st house, in an enemy's sign, strength 52 out of 100 (moderate). These take their colour from whatever they sit with rather than carrying an agenda of their own.
